# Ampere v2 — development handoff notes

Audience: humans joining or reviewing the v2 redevelopment, and the person
dispatching agents. Agents themselves should start from `AGENTS.md`.

## The three documents

1. `DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md` — decisions, architecture, phases, traps. The
   source of truth; changes to decisions go through its decision log.
2. `WORK_ITEMS.md` — agent-sized items for Phases 0–1, the working
   agreement, and the status table (updated at merge, not by agents).
3. `AGENTS.md` — ground rules and environment for any coding agent
   (Claude Code reads it via the `CLAUDE.md` symlink; Codex reads it
   natively).

## Dispatching work

- Each item is dispatched to one agent, on one branch (`w0.2-repo-hygiene`
  style), producing one PR. Respect the **Depends** lines in WORK_ITEMS.md;
  items with no unmet dependencies can run in parallel.
- Claude Code: point a session/agent at the item id — the `work-item`
  project skill carries the procedure. Codex: `codex exec` with a
  self-contained prompt; see `.claude/skills/delegate-codex/SKILL.md`.
- Agents do not push unless told to; review happens locally or on pushed
  branches at Peter's discretion.
- **Harness trap (hit and solved 2026-09-01)**: subagent worktrees may be
  created from the *session-start* commit rather than current HEAD. Every
  dispatch prompt must state the intended base commit and the recovery:
  verify the tree is clean and HEAD is a strict ancestor of the target
  (`git merge-base --is-ancestor HEAD <target>`), then branch directly
  from the target; STOP and report otherwise. Both W1.5/W1.6 agents
  recovered cleanly with this.
- **Harness trap (hit and solved 2026-09-03)**: `!`-prefixed commands
  typed by Peter run in the orchestrating session's *persistent shell
  cwd*. If that shell was last in an agent worktree, a `git merge` typed
  there silently no-ops ("Already up to date"). The orchestrator must
  return its shell to the main checkout before handing over any git
  command to run.

## Review checklist (per PR)

- Acceptance criteria evidenced in the report, not just claimed.
- Diff stays inside the item's scope; frozen legacy modules untouched
  (`ampere/data`, `ampere/models`, `ampere/infer`) unless the item says so.
- No run outputs, binaries, or checkpoints; British English in prose.
- From Phase 1 on: conformance suite green; contract changes carry a
  decision-log entry.
- On merge: update the WORK_ITEMS.md status table.

## Environment

- **pixi is the supported route** (W0.8, landed): from a clean clone with
  only `pixi` installed, `pixi install -e dev` sets up the daily-use
  environment (Python 3.13 + the `dev`, `zeus`, `extinction` extras).
  pyproject.toml's `[tool.pixi.*]` tables wrap `[project.dependencies]` /
  `[project.optional-dependencies]` — pixi never duplicates a dependency
  pyproject.toml already declares, it just adds Python (from conda-forge)
  and installs `ampere` itself as an editable PyPI dependency with the
  extras a given feature needs. `pixi.lock` is committed so environments
  are reproducible; `.pixi/` (the installed environments themselves) is
  not, and is gitignored.
  - Common tasks: `pixi run test` (fast: `tests/test_imports.py`),
    `pixi run test-all` (nine suites since **W5.26** — core, results,
    conformance, backends, inference, examples (since W2.11), m2, and
    (since W5.26) interferometry and astrometry — in ONE pytest process;
    **this is the main gate**, and `test-phase1` remains the older
    core+results+conformance subset; the individual `test-core`,
    `test-results`, `test-backends`, `test-inference`, `test-examples` and
    `conformance` tasks exist too), `pixi run test-fast` (W5.18: the local
    pre-commit run — the contract-and-backend tier of `test-all`: core,
    results, conformance, backends, inference and the M2 agreement rows,
    minus the `study` rows, the SBI-training classes and two slow results
    classes, with the three example suites left to `test-all` and CI's
    `studies` group; **since W6.10 `test-all` and `test-fast` run `-n 4`**
    (`-n 0` for a serial run; the audit table is in "The merged gate" below); **ruled by Peter 2026-09-27** on the measurement;
    2 min 55 s for 2879 passed / 425 skipped / 244 deselected in `dev` — the
    task's own comment in `pyproject.toml` has the durations and the
    reasons),
    `pixi run test-characterisation` (legacy still works),
    `pixi run lint`, `pixi run format-check`, `pixi run typecheck`,
    `pixi run docs` (repaired at W2.11 — it builds), `pixi run bench` (the
    tracked benchmarks; writes `benchmark.json`, which CI uploads as a run
    artefact) and `pixi run scaling` (the on-demand 10³–10⁵ GP scaling
    demonstration, deliberately in no gate). Since W0.10, plain
    `pixi run <task>` equals `-e dev`.
  - Other environments (`pixi run -e <env> <task>`): `test-py312` /
    `test-py313` / `test-py314` (the CI matrix, one Python each); `sbi`
    (since W3.2 the `dev` set plus the `torch` feature and the `sbi`
    extra — sbi 0.27, torch, pyro — and a real gate of its own:
    `pixi run -e sbi test-all`, plus `pixi run -e sbi typecheck`); `torch` /
    `jax` (the backend environments — real gates since W2.4/W2.5:
    `pixi run -e torch test-all`, `pixi run -e jax test-all`, and each
    backend package is typechecked with its real types only in its own
    environment). Since W2.11 both `torch` and `sbi` resolve torch from
    PyTorch's **CPU** index, so neither pulls ~2 GB of CUDA runtime onto a
    CPU-only runner; this changes nothing about what `pip install
    "ampere[torch]"` gives a user. **Run `test-all` gates one at a time** —
    two concurrently exhaust a 13 GB machine. Measured on this machine at
    W5.26 (2026-09-24, nine suites including `tests/interferometry` and
    `tests/astrometry`, the studies' numpy-path sampling rows skipped
    outside `dev`): dev 33 min, torch 55 min, jax 33 min, sbi 62 min — the
    orchestrator's merged-gate baselines. Before W5.26 (seven suites, the
    study rows everywhere): dev 27 min, torch 62, jax 45, sbi 69. The two
    new suites cost about 3 min in dev, 7 on torch, 4 on jax and 6 on sbi
    of those figures; the study rows they replace cost about 10 min of
    each modern-backend leg (`tests/m2` + `tests/results/test_population.py`:
    18.5 min in dev where they run, 8.7 min on torch where only the
    backend-agreement rows remain).
  - **CI's `suites` matrix** (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`): since W5.26, a
    blocking job per environment-and-group cell, each producing a check
    named "new-namespace suites (`<environment>`, `<group>`)" — `dev` (the
    `suites` job) and, in the `backend-suites` job's matrix, `torch`, `jax`
    and, since W3.7, `sbi` and, since W5.14, `nested`. `group` is
    `core`/`backends`/`studies` (`pyproject.toml`'s `test-group-core` /
    `test-group-backends` / `test-group-studies` tasks, each a named subset
    of `test-all`'s suites — see that task's comment for exactly which
    suites and why), so what used to be one forty-minute `test-all` step per
    environment is now three smaller, independently-reported steps: a red
    row in one group no longer hides the other two groups' results in the
    same leg. `bench` runs once per environment, gated to the `core` cell,
    not once per group (`torch`/`jax` only; deliberately not repeated for
    `sbi`/`nested` since either would only re-exercise another leg's own
    benchmarks under another name — see the job's comment). `sbi`'s install
    is cached the same way as `torch`'s (`setup-pixi`'s `cache: true`). The
    weekly/on-demand `sbi-characterisation` job is unrelated: it exercises
    the *legacy* `ampere.infer.sbi` flow, not the new-namespace `sbi` gate,
    and stays non-blocking.
  - **W4.10 — `typecheck` split out of the backend legs.** Since W4.10,
    `pixi run -e <env> typecheck` for `torch`/`jax`/`sbi` runs in its own
    matrix job, `backend-typecheck` (check name
    "typecheck (pyrefly, `<environment>`)"), independent of
    `backend-suites` (no `needs:` between them) — the two run concurrently
    rather than one serialising in front of the other, so the slow `sbi`
    leg's critical path in `backend-suites` is `test-all` alone. This was
    W3.7's finding: the `sbi` leg's local reference time (14–20 min) sits
    above the `torch` leg's (12–17 min), and splitting `typecheck` out was
    one of the two cheap levers considered there without widening that
    item's scope to touch test budgets. `dev`'s own `typecheck` job is
    unchanged (it already ran on its own).
  - **W4.10 — path gating.** A `changes` job runs first and computes, from
    a pull request's changed files, which of six buckets are touched —
    `run_dev`, `run_torch`, `run_jax`, `run_sbi`, `run_nested`
    (W5.14), `run_docs` — using
    `.github/scripts/path_filters.py` as the single executable source of
    the rule table (reproduced as a comment at the top of `ci.yml` and
    below). Every job in the workflow always runs; a bucket controls
    whether that job's *steps* do real work or print a one-line skip note,
    so an unaffected leg's check still reports **success**, never
    "skipped" or "expected" — the latter is what a top-level
    `on.push.paths`/`on.pull_request.paths` filter would produce (the
    workflow never triggers at all on an unaffected PR, so a required
    check named in branch protection never gets a status and the merge
    button hangs waiting for it). Push to master, `workflow_dispatch` and
    `schedule` runs are never gated — path gating only narrows a
    *pull_request* diff's turnaround time, never what lands on master or
    what a human explicitly asked to run.

    The path-filter table (buckets are exclusive; a path is classified by
    the first rule that matches — see the script's module docstring for
    the authoritative version):

    | Path pattern | Runs |
    | --- | --- |
    | `ampere/inference/_blackjax.py`, `tests/inference/test_blackjax.py` (W5.14; matched *before* the CORE row below) | dev, jax (blackjax installs into the existing jax environment, so it rides that leg) |
    | `ampere/inference/_nested.py`, `tests/inference/test_nested.py` (W5.14; matched *before* the row below) | dev, nested |
    | `pyproject.toml`, `pixi.lock`, `.github/**`, `ampere/core/**`, `ampere/results/**`, `ampere/inference/**`, and the shared test suites (`tests/{core,results,inference,conformance,m2,interferometry,astrometry,benchmarks,scaling,gpu,characterisation}/**` — `interferometry`/`astrometry` joined W5.26) | everything: dev, torch, jax, sbi, nested, docs |
    | `ampere/backends/torch/**`, `tests/backends/*torch*` | torch, sbi (sbi's environment installs torch too) |
    | `ampere/backends/jax/**`, `tests/backends/*jax*` | jax |
    | `ampere/backends/reference/**`, remaining `tests/backends/**` | dev only |
    | `examples/sbi/**` | dev, sbi |
    | `examples/interferometry/**` (W4.4, not landed at W4.10 — a marked slot) | dev, torch, jax, sbi (conservative; W4.4 should narrow this to the backend(s) each example file actually exercises) |
    | `examples/**` (remainder), `tests/examples/**` | dev only |
    | `docs/**`, any `*.md` | dev, docs |
    | anything unrecognised | everything (same as the first row) |

    Locally: `python .github/scripts/path_filters.py --paths <files...>`
    (or `--stdin` with a newline-separated list) prints the bucket for each
    path and the resulting job list — the same thing the `changes` job
    prints in its own log. This is also how the item's three synthetic
    diffs were evidenced (W4.10's report).
  - **W4.10 — `actionlint`.** `pixi run -e dev actionlint` (a conda-forge
    binary, alongside `pandoc` in the `dev` feature) validates
    `.github/workflows/*.yml`; CI's `actionlint` job runs the same task,
    ungated (cheap, and useful on every PR — not only ones touching a
    workflow file). Every `uses:` in `ci.yml` is pinned to a full commit
    SHA with a `# vX.Y` comment (pinact style), and
    `.github/dependabot.yml` enables the `github-actions` ecosystem
    (weekly) so a version bump arrives as a reviewable PR updating both the
    SHA and its comment together.
  - **Required-check names for branch protection** (unchanged names keep
    their existing branch-protection entries; new ones need adding):
    `lint + format-check`, `actionlint`,
    `typecheck (pyrefly, new namespaces)`, `test (py312)`, `test (py313)`,
    `test (py314)`,
    `typecheck (pyrefly, torch)`, `typecheck (pyrefly, jax)`,
    `typecheck (pyrefly, sbi)`, `typecheck (pyrefly, nested)`
    (nested new at W5.14 — needs adding to branch protection), and, since
    W5.26 replaced the five single "new-namespace suites (`<environment>`)"
    checks with one per environment-and-group cell, fifteen
    "new-namespace suites (`<environment>`, `<group>`)" checks —
    `<environment>` one of `dev`/`torch`/`jax`/`sbi`/`nested`, `<group>` one
    of `core`/`backends`/`studies` (all fifteen need adding to branch
    protection in place of the five they replace), plus
    `docs build`, `minimal install (no extras)`. (`path filters` — the
    `changes` job itself — and the weekly/on-demand
    `characterisation suite (with sbi extra)` are not required checks: the
    former is plumbing every other job depends on, not a signal in its own
    right, and the latter is deliberately non-blocking, as before W4.10.)
- Plain-pip alternative: `pip install -e ".[dev]"`, Python ≥ 3.12 (raised 2026-09-22).
- Conda env `ampere` (Python 3.13) has an editable install pointing at the
  main checkout — worktree-based work that needs importing its own changes
  should use pixi or `pip install -e .` into a fresh env instead.
- `pytest tests/characterisation` (or `pixi run test-characterisation`) is
  the "legacy still works" gate; run it before merging anything that
  touches shared files.

```
Merge branch 'w5.30-housekeeping-iii'

W5.30 — Phase 5 housekeeping III: the flat-population cap and the
hierarchical bijection (Sonnet-authored, Fable-reviewed).

(a) MAX_FLAT_MEMBERS's docstring and Population's refusal cite the
measured figures (hierarchical_population.md §5: 46 ms per lnprior at
100 flat members, 373 ms at 1000, 1.3 ms as one plate; about 60 ms at
the cap) in place of the wrong ones, and ampere.core.settings — one
dataclass, one field, flat_population_cap = "refuse" | "warn", an
override() that restores on exit — turns the refusal into an
AmpereFlatPopulationWarning for a user who accepts the cost; the limit
does not move. (b) _default_bijection_for_hierarchical infers for a
shape family whose support does not move with its shape (gamma, beta,
lognorm: the base-class _get_support) when at least one shape argument
is a constant, and keeps refusing truncnorm and any family all of whose
shape arguments are hyperparameter references; an explicit bijection
still wins; the horseshoe helper's declaration infers the same Log
without its keyword. (c) replace_observations carries
problem.populations into the replica — the union of the populations
given to FittingProblem and those on the collection, since the former
never reach datasets.populations — closing the same silent drop W5.28
(a) closed for the shared set.
```

*W5.28 merge message* (`~/.cache/ampere-gates/w5.28-merge-message.txt`):

```
Merge branch 'w5.28-housekeeping-ii'

W5.28 — Phase 5 housekeeping II: the owed list from the wave-3 and
wave-4 reviews, eleven lettered items, each landed with a test or
recorded as a documented decision (Sonnet-authored, Fable-reviewed).

Landed: (a) replace_observations carries the shared parameter set and
its label into the replica, so a population-level SBC replay is no
longer silently independent; (c) coupling_matrix_summary, the
summarise-side helper that reassembles RotationCoupling's B per draw
and hands it to arviz.summary instead of the angle that is identified
only up to a relabelling; (d) the reference QuasisepGP.condition(at=)
accepts a multi-axis container as DenseGP does; (e) the gridded-solver
mixin is public, GriddedSolver, the image study's extension point;
(f) HilbertSpaceGP.condition(at=None) reuses the training block rather
than forming the (N, m) basis twice; (g) _concatenate refuses a group
mismatch by name in either direction for any group; (i) with_shrinkage
registers the rebuilt parameters through register_parameter rather than
writing a ParameterSet into __dict__; (j) SBIEngine.calibrate() reseeds
torch (and numpy's legacy global) as run() does and restores the state
on exit, and the test suite's hand-rolled workaround for that leak is
gone; (k) one tests/conftest.py puts the repository root on sys.path
once, replacing six copies, and records why tests/ stays a non-package.
Documented decisions: (b) sbc ranks the posterior group only, because
the derived groups have no truth simulation.parameters can supply; (h)
an empty TrainingSet.contexts is deliberate parity with observations
written under observe=False.
```

*Decision-log rows (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after W5.12's row, in this order)*:

| Tier-1 nested sampling behind extras (W5.14, first agent) | **`nautilus` and `ultranest` ship as `NautilusEngine`/`UltranestEngine` over a shared `ampere/inference/_nested.py`, one extra per sampler** (`inference_extensions_memo.md` §6 tier 1, §10's 2026-09-10 ruling; merged 2026-09-22 at `MERGE_HASH`), joining `DynestyEngine` on `prior_transform`. `inference.md` §10's portability claim is checked by three libraries of the same kind for the first time and **nothing in the contract changed to admit them**: both consume `prior_transform` and `log_likelihood` and nothing else. Four consequences recorded. (a) **`results.md` §9's weighted-draw rule is one implementation for all three** — `dynesty.utils.resample_equal` on the engine's own `resample` stream — because three nested samplers must not be able to produce three slightly different posteriors from the same dead points; each library's own equal-weight output is unused, since each draws from its library's randomness rather than the problem's seed. (b) **W5.0's carried question is answered: `ampere_evidence_method` names the method *family*, not the engine** — `"nested_sampling"` for all three — because `ampere_engine` already names the engine and what a reader needs from the second attribute is whether two archived evidences were estimated the same way. (c) **nautilus reports no evidence uncertainty**, so ampere records the importance-sampling estimate `1/sqrt(n_eff)` over the library's own Kish effective size and says so in `ampere_nautilus_log_z_err_source`; it also refuses a one-dimensional problem by name, which is why the closed-form linear-Gaussian evidence case is run at **two** free parameters rather than W3.6's one. (d) **Design horizon (g)'s cost record is `engine_evaluations`**, which `Engine.finish` already writes for every run, beside each sampler's own likelihood-call count — no new engine-neutral attribute, as §10's ruling that (e) to (g) need no contract change today requires. Two helpers became shared rather than duplicated: `engine.default_live_points` (out of `_dynesty.py`) and `engine.global_seed` (out of `_zeus.py`, because ultranest draws from numpy's process-global generator exactly as zeus does — `inference.md` §12's thread-safety limitation now has two owners). Packaging: `nautilus`/`ultranest`/`blackjax` extras with 1:1 pixi features; **one** new pixi environment, `nested` (dev's feature set, numpy-only), with a path-gated CI leg on both backend matrices, while **blackjax folds into the existing `jax` environment** — a jax-only driver has no other environment it could run in. The **engine battery** (`tests/inference/test_nested.py`) is the memo's §6 prescription made executable, with dynesty in it as the control; measured against analytic log Z = −13.0684: dynesty −0.15 σ, nautilus −0.26 σ, ultranest +1.46 σ. |

| The rest of tier 1: variational guide families and a second gradient library (W5.14, successor agent) | **`VIEngine` gains the guide families `laplace` and `flow` on both routes, and `BlackjaxEngine(problem, method=...)` lands jax-only with MCLMC and Pathfinder** (merged 2026-09-22 at `MERGE_HASH`; Opus-authored across two sessions, the second continuing the first's branch under the 2026-09-22 hand-off rule). Three things the inference-extensions memo's §2 did not anticipate, each found by running the code rather than reading the libraries. (a) §2.2's "Laplace is a new entry in `GUIDE_FAMILIES` and nothing else" is false: in **both** libraries the autoguide is a `Delta` during optimisation, so SVI fits the MAP location and the Gaussian exists only afterwards — pyro's `laplace_approximation()` returns an `AutoMultivariateNormal`, numpyro's `get_posterior(params)` builds one from the Hessian on demand — and a driver that drew from what SVI fitted would return the mode `draws` times. (b) A flow guide cannot be started with `init_loc_fn` (both libraries ignore it) and in ampere's unconstrained coordinates it must be started somehow, a parameter with an unbounded prior being its own coordinate: measured at `norm ≈ 1.1` after 4000 steps against a posterior at `2.01 ± 0.10`, against mean-field's 600 steps. Both routes move the flow's **base** to the same seeded prior draw through `get_base_dist`, the hook both libraries define; pyro's autoregressive network is additionally built in float64 explicitly, `lowering.md` §10.1 forbidding `set_default_dtype`. (c) `ampere_approximation`'s meaning is settled by the blackjax pair: Pathfinder writes `"pathfinder"` with a per-draw `proposal_log_density` (blackjax returns the density beside its draws, so §5.2b costs only the coordinate change), while **MCLMC writes `"none"` despite being an unadjusted chain with a discretisation bias** — the key answers "do chain diagnostics apply?", and for a Markov chain they do; the bias is recorded under the engine's own names and measured by the battery's SBC. Neither blackjax method writes the engine-neutral evidence triple, and the battery asserts its *absence*: MCLMC samples an unnormalised density, and Pathfinder's ELBO is one L-BFGS path's lower bound of unknown tightness, which under a nested sampler's attribute name would invite a meaningless comparison. `ampere_approximation`'s vocabulary widens by `"laplace"` and `"normalising_flow"` with no reader change (every consumer tests `!= "none"`), **`results.md` §9's example list amended at review** to say so and to name the evidence triple's absence as a state; the new `ampere_vi_guide_parameters` records which fitted parameters a run left on the engine — `"none"` for a flow, whose fit is a network's weights and whose `guide_loc`/`guide_scale`/`guide_scale_tril` all stay `None`. Two floors refused by name in ampere's words before a library raises: a flow below two free parameters, and MCLMC below two (the isokinetic dynamics decorrelate a *direction*). CI gains a `BLACKJAX` path bucket gated to the **jax** leg rather than a leg of its own, blackjax installing into the existing jax environment. **Carried**: whether MCLMC's `"none"` should instead be a named unadjusted family. |

*Decision-log row (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after the Python-floor row)*: the agent's text, verbatim from its report, with `MERGE_HASH`:

| `Population`: hierarchy declared at composition time, and the joint population fit (W5.12) | **Decided 2026-09-22 (merged at `MERGE_HASH`; Opus-authored, Fable-reviewed), discharging Peter's ruling of 2026-09-03 on `hierarchical_population.md` §11 Q2 (`Population` lands with Phase 5) and the joint-fit half of Q5.** `ampere.core.Population` is the hierarchical counterpart of `Tie`: `ParameterSet.merge(..., populations=[...])` adds the hyperpriors as one further top-level component and gives every component named in `over` its own draw under the member's bare local name, so N independently written per-object models compose into a population fit unmodified — which is the cost gap H-1 named. It ships **two layouts declaring one density**: `layout="plate"` (the default) produces H-2's array-valued parameter and per-element `Binding.index` routing, lowers to a real `numpyro`/`pyro` plate on the native path, and is unbounded; `layout="flat"` produces the tie-based pattern `parameters.md` §9 documents and is refused above `MAX_FLAT_MEMBERS` (128), which turns §7's measured O(number of `Parameter` objects) prior cost into a guardrail. The merge's signature change is additive and keyword-only, so no existing call and no conformance row moved. `DatasetCollection.plate(...)` lifts `inference.md` limitation 17.6; a population is refused over a component merged as a `ParameterMapping`, because a composite's merged names are qualified and a bare-named element would never reach a leaf — a plate of datasets declares its population over the models those datasets name. `ParameterMapping.distribute` now takes an element binding's index **in the value's own array type**: both backends' realisations route native values through the merge's table, and the previous `numpy.asarray` coercion raised on a torch tensor requiring grad (recorded in `inference.md` §10a). Amended: `parameters.md` §8/§9, `inference.md` §9/§10a/17.6, `hierarchical_population.md` §9 H-1 and §11 Q2/Q5. Conformance: `tests/conformance/test_population.py`; recovery: `tests/inference/test_population_nuts.py`. |

*Decision-log row (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after the W5.10 row):*

| The sparsity guard on a sum of noise components, and the M2 scenario that needs two length scales (W5.8) | **`ampere.core.regularised_horseshoe` is the recommended prior for any `Sum` of noise terms, and `ampere.core.with_shrinkage` is the one hook that puts it on a kernel** (merged 2026-09-18, `MERGE_HASH`; Opus-authored, Fable-reviewed). The declaration is the horseshoe's global-local chain exactly — `τ ~ C⁺(0, τ₀)`, `s_j | τ ~ C⁺(0, τ)`, `a_j | s_j ~ N⁺(0, s_j)` — and the multiplicative `s_j = τ λ_j` is written as **one** declaration rather than a product, which it may be because the half-Cauchy is a scale family; that is what lets the whole prior be expressed with `HierarchicalPrior`, which references a parameter by name and has no product node, with **no change to any existing class**. **The hook could not be avoided and the reason is structural**: a `ParameterSet` refuses, on each registration, a reference not already declared — which is what stops a dangling reference reaching a sampler — and a kernel registers a summand's `amplitude` in its own `__init__` before anything a horseshoe would give it to reference, so the scale levels must be registered ahead of the amplitudes and only something that rebuilds the whole set can do that. `with_shrinkage` is **functional** (the kernel it is given is unchanged — verified at review: the original's spec hash and names are untouched and the shrunk kernel's hash moves), `_Composite.__init__` is untouched, and a kernel built without it has exactly the parameters, spec and hash it had before; `FAMILY`, `terms`, capabilities and axes are untouched, so it lowers unchanged and the extra names never reach a child because `_child_values` selects on the `label.` prefix. **What is *not* Piironen & Vehtari's**: their slab, `λ̃_j² = c²λ_j²/(c² + τ²λ_j²)`, is a deterministic function of two sampled parameters and §4.1 declares parameters and priors, not deterministic nodes — so `tail="regularised"` (the default) gives the declarable form of the same guard, a `gamma(a=½, scale=τ)` local level that keeps the horseshoe's `s^{-½}` spike and replaces the Cauchy tail with an exponential one, and a `Derived` parameter node is recorded as what would close the gap. It also lowers, where the plain horseshoe does not: **`halfcauchy` is in neither backend's §3.2 table**, so `tail="cauchy"` and the global level need a `register_lowering` row, recorded in `lowering.md` §3.2.1 along with the non-centred parameterisation NUTS wants and why spike-and-slab stays out (§12 Q1: its indicator is discrete). **Validated by the M2 extension the plan's Phase-5 bullet names**: `many_lines`, five narrow lines confined to 0.860–0.870 µm plus a smooth continuum error a hundred times broader — kept **out** of `SCENARIOS`, so `run_study` still defaults to the four and the milestone's figures, tables and CI session are unchanged. Measured at 200 points: standard 35.70 posterior widths (1/4 covered), stationary Matérn **2.84 (3/4)** — the first place in this study a flexible likelihood is required to *fail* M2's own 1.5 threshold, and it fails for a structural reason, the kernel being the wrong *shape* — W5.7's warped Matérn 0.92 (4/4) and W4.5's `Sum` 0.78 (4/4), all three localising inside the line band. Every threshold is a margin, not a number (W4.5's `_period_margin` precedent), and the shrinkage thresholds sit a third below the worst of three run seeds because the horseshoe's three levels give the posterior a funnel an ensemble sampler explores unevenly. SBC over refits with the deviation injected pins the contrast the visibility precedent pins (standard 0.00/0.80 against warped 0.80/0.90 at nominal 0.90), not an absolute coverage, because at ten refits an exactly calibrated fit returns 8/10 more than a quarter of the time. Cross-backend: torch 0.130 and jax 0.085 on a median against a 0.5 tolerance, with NUTS reaching all six warp variables through `ampere.core.realise`. `likelihoods.md` §6 (discharging its line 889 promise) and §13, `parameters.md` §9, `lowering.md` §3 amended. Conformance untouched and green. |

*Decision-log row (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after the W5.11 row):*

| **Amortisation over the observation context: the `ContextPrior` (W5.10)** | **`simulate_many(context=...)`'s reserved slot is filled, and no layout hash moved to fill it** (merged 2026-09-18, `MERGE_HASH`; Opus-authored, Fable-reviewed; one fix at review). A budget drawn the ordinary way uses the observed container's own uncertainties, so the posterior it trains is amortised over noise *realisations* but not over noise *levels*. `ampere.core.simulate.ContextPrior` — `draw(rng, observed) -> ObservationContext` and `describe()`, structural like `Executor` — is what varies them, with three shipped instances answering the three ways an uncertainty pattern is actually known: `ScaledSigma` (scaled copies of the observed pattern), `SigmaArchive` (real error arrays) and `SignalToNoise` (a parametric S/N model, the one needing no observed σ at all). **The σ reaches exactly one place**: `Dataset.contextual_observed` replaces that dataset's observed container for that draw, so every family's `sample` receives `NoiseParams` built from the context and the drawn observation carries its own error bars — a draw at a different noise *level*, not a rescaling applied afterwards, and **no `LikelihoodFamily.sample` signature moved**. That is also why `encoding.md` needed no new column and **every layout hash is unchanged**: the packing already carries each sample's `log σ` and whitened value, so a network reading `unpack` sees the context of the observation it is conditioned on, and the reserved `context` group stays width 0 for a context that is genuinely not already a column. The opt-in second route is FiLM — a per-dataset conditioning vector from the masked mean and spread of `log σ` and `log N_valid`, zero-initialised so that switching it on is the identity before the first gradient step. **Contexts are drawn per simulation on their own `"<stream>.context"` sub-stream, spawned by index** (`inference.md` §12): a budget's θ and its noise are bit-for-bit what they were before the item existed, and draw *i*'s context is independent of the chunking — they are materialised a chunk at a time so a budget larger than memory never holds them all. Recorded on every `Simulation` (failures included), in the batch's `provenance['simulation_context']`, in a training set's optional `context` group and root attrs, and in an SBI run's `ampere_sbi_context`/`ampere_sbi_context_hash`; the σ arrays are **not** stored twice, because a drawn observation carries them. **The prior is also an ingredient of the artefact cache key** (`artefact_key(context=…)`, `results.md` §9), and that is not book-keeping: a budget drawn under a context prior trains a different network from one drawn at the observed uncertainties, and two priors covering different ranges train two more, so without it all three computed the same key and the store served whichever was trained first — §7's stale artefact arriving by the one door the key exists to shut, and something the recorded attrs can only diagnose after the fact (found at review). It is the prior's digest rather than its description (an archive of real error arrays does not belong in every sidecar), it is `None` for a budget drawn without one, and it is omitted from `ingredients()` rather than written as `null`, so **every digest minted before W5.10 is unchanged** — verified byte-for-byte against master's own `artefacts.py` (`3af107dc1797effaf1cc51c626312838` either side), and W3.12's pinned `_BASE_COMMIT_NPE_DIGEST` still holds untouched. **SBC per observation is the check that the prior covered the observation at hand** and `calibrate(context=...)` is how it is asked: inheriting the run's prior by default, taking one of its own otherwise, and recording which distribution each verdict is about. Measured on a 1500-draw NPE under `ScaledSigma(0.5, 2.0)`: TARP area-to-curve `-0.007` at a ×1.5 rescale the prior covers and `-0.046` at a ×12 it does not, pinned as inequalities at about half the separation. Three refusals by name rather than guesses: `observe=False` (the context *is* the level the observations are drawn at), `native=True` (the native path draws its noise from the realisation's own σ, which a per-draw container σ does not reach — the loop runs instead and the provenance says so), and a problem with a **joint noise group** (a group's σ is read from the first of its datasets and its channels are drawn in one correlated call, so what a per-dataset context means for the cross-covariance is a design question, not a detail). `SignalToNoise` shapes its σ from the **observed** container's magnitudes, because a context is drawn before the forward model runs: under `reference="values"` every draw gets the observation's own fractional-error shape at one drawn S/N, not its own simulated signal's — stated in the docstring; a prior that tracked each draw would need `draw` to receive the predicted containers, which is a protocol change and a later item. `PROVENANCE_SCHEMA_VERSION` unchanged at 8 and `TRAINING_SET_SCHEMA_VERSION` at 1: the context attrs are engine-specific extras and the `context` group is optional, exactly as `observations` was at W2.8. |

*Decision-log row (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after the W5.22 row):*

| **The encoding's axis identity: a physical-type code per coordinate column (W5.11, D7)** | **`encoding.md` §3 gains a tenth column group, `axis_identity`, and every layout hash moves once** (merged 2026-09-18, `MERGE_HASH`; Opus-authored, Fable-reviewed). §9 item 6 recorded that the packing aligns axes by *position* — column `k` is whichever axis a kind declares in position `k` — so a collection mixing an `Image` (column 0 is `x`, an angle on the sky) with a `VisibilitySet` (column 0 is `u`, a spatial frequency) showed a network two unrelated quantities in one input slot. The lift is **one small integer per coordinate column, taken from the axis's unit** through `astropy.units.get_physical_type`, which is the one statement of what a coordinate is that every container already carries: a `Spectrum` in `um` and one in `Hz` hold different quantities in the same column and the codes now say so. The thirteen-entry table (`AXIS_TYPE_CODES`) is **contract** — the numbers are frozen into every layout's hash and every trained network's input, so a code may be added as the next free integer but never renumbered or reused — and `0` (`absent`, the padded column of a dataset with fewer axes than the widest) is deliberately distinct from `1` (`unknown`, an axis whose physical type the table cannot name). The **one** quantity astropy cannot name is `spatial frequency`: `u` in wavelengths is a baseline over a wavelength and reads `dimensionless`, `u` in `rad**-1` reads `unknown` (a radian is an *angle* to astropy, and the reciprocal of an angle has no name), so a container kind declares it by putting `rad**-1` in its `AxisSpec.equivalent_units` and an axis matching either spelling is coded 8 — while a `u` given in metres is a baseline **length** and is coded 3, which is the honest answer. `DatasetLayout.axis_codes` puts the codes in the layout and therefore in the hash; `unpack` exposes the group on `Unpacked` and every `DatasetView` and `features` carries it, so no embedding wrapper needed changing. **`ENCODING_VERSION` 1 → 2, and every layout hash written under version 1 moves exactly once** — measured on the module docstring's one-dataset `Spectrum` layout: `e73db8a8032273f4c511142f1459c51c` (17 columns, version 1) → `822f4c900af7a854488b2044db598e26` (18 columns, version 2), the old value taken from `dd79aa0`'s own code rather than reconstructed. The version is what lets both doors refuse a pre-W5.11 record **by name** — `EncodingLayout.from_dict` and `ampere.results.training.append_training_set`, sharing one message through `axis_identity_complaint` — rather than report a hash that does not match. Deliberately left open, and narrower than what was closed: the codes name a *physical type*, not a role, so two `length` axes of one collection (a wavelength and a baseline) still share a code, and a kind that wants them distinguished needs a new code rather than a new mechanism. |

*Status row (WORK_ITEMS.md, after W5.7's):*

| W5.23 | merged 2026-09-17 at `MERGE_HASH` (Sonnet-authored, Fable-reviewed — the self-verifying `get_by_digest` (the sidecar's recorded ingredients must hash back to the digest it is filed under, which is exactly `ArtefactKey.digest`'s definition), the served/mismatch attrs and the untouched default path read; the orchestrator's re-run of `tests/results/test_artefacts.py` and the serving rows in sbi: 63 passed in 13 s; no fixes needed; merged by Peter; the agent's runs: sbi `tests/inference/test_sbi.py` 163/2 in 294 s, `test_artefacts.py` 59/0, import sweep 105/2, lint/format/typecheck clean; the merged-wave gate to be recorded with W5.9; the agent: 247 k tokens / 158 tool uses / 47 min). Landed: `SBIEngine(cache=..., serve_artefact=<digest>)` — the ordinary key still computed and recorded, the named entry restored regardless through `ArtefactStore.get_by_digest` (a `(artefact, ingredients)` pair, or `None` with an `ArtefactCacheWarning` for a missing, unreadable, hand-edited or corrupt entry), a miss refused by name as `EngineError` rather than falling back to training, `serve_artefact` without `cache` refused at construction; attrs `sbi_artefact_served` and `sbi_artefact_mismatch` (field-wise, from the factored `ArtefactStore.diff_ingredients`, empty when they agree) beside `sbi_cache_key`, with an `ArtefactCacheWarning` naming the differing fields; `results.md` §9 documents all three (`sbi_cache_key` had been written since W3.5 but never listed); `docs/source/sbi.rst`'s cache section gains the explicit route; 9 store rows and 4 engine rows (the served-and-calibrated row differs on `budget`, the file's established one-ingredient case, since its fixtures have no HSGP problem). Carried: **`SBIEngine.calibrate()` does not reseed torch as `run()` does** (the agent found SBC/TARP thresholds in `test_sbi.py` sensitive to test order when a preceding test samples a posterior; its own row saves and restores `torch.get_rng_state()` around `calibrate()` as a local mitigation) — a follow-up for the next housekeeping item, W3.15's pattern applied to `calibrate()`. |

*Decision-log row (DEVELOPMENT_PLAN.md §2, after the W5.7 row):*

| An explicit route to serve one named cached SBI artefact, with the mismatch recorded (W5.23) | **`SBIEngine(cache=..., serve_artefact=<digest>)` restores the artefact filed under that digest regardless of whether it matches the run's own computed key, and the run's attrs say so** (merged MERGE_DATE, `MERGE_HASH`; Sonnet-authored, Fable-reviewed; from Peter's note of 2026-09-16 on W5.4's hashing). The content-addressed cache is unchanged and its rule stands: a posterior trained at one `basis_size` is a miss for another by construction, because two runs at different approximation parameters are not the same model. A user who accepts that risk names the artefact explicitly rather than getting a fuzzy match: `ArtefactStore.get_by_digest` looks an entry up by its filename digest alone and trusts it only by *self-verification* — the sidecar's recorded ingredients must hash back to the digest they are filed under, which is `ArtefactKey.digest`'s own definition — so a hand-edited or corrupt entry is refused with an `ArtefactCacheWarning` exactly as `get` refuses, and a missing digest is an `EngineError` rather than a fall-back to training. The run's attrs carry `sbi_cache_key` (the digest this run's settings would have used — written since W3.5, listed in `results.md` §9 only now), `sbi_artefact_served` and `sbi_artefact_mismatch` (field-wise, from the one factored `ArtefactStore.diff_ingredients`, empty when they agree), with a warning naming the differing fields; three engine-specific `extra=` attrs, none schema-counted, so no `PROVENANCE_SCHEMA_VERSION` bump. The default path is bitwise unchanged (W3.15's reproducibility row green, unmodified). |

**Operations notes for the orchestrator**: one five-suite gate at a time, always through the `flock`; run long gates detached (`nohup … &`) and wait with a background `until` loop rather than polling in the foreground; **never edit, format or check out source files in the main checkout while a gate runs there** (a half-written module crashed a subprocess test on 2026-09-09; markdown is safe); an agent that `cd`s to the main checkout can edit its files even though the isolation guard blocks git there — tell agents to stay in their worktree; after a lockfile change, `pixi install -e <env>` in the main checkout; the merged-master gate is the one that counts; parallel agents appending decision-log rows conflict at the table's last line — keep both rows, keep the table contiguous; agent worktrees under `.claude/worktrees/` may hold their own `.pixi` (2 GB) — remove a merged agent's worktree with `git worktree remove --force --force`; finished agents linger in the task list until dismissed with `TaskStop`; an agent that "stops to wait" for a gate is resumed with `SendMessage` and told to poll; a session rate limit kills agents mid-flight but their committed work survives — resume them from their branches.


## The merged gate: CI is the gate of record

*(W6.10, ruled 2026-10-02 under D4. The status-table convention below applies from the next merged row on; earlier rows keep the local five-suite summary lines they were written with.)*

**Before the merge, the scoped runs.** The orchestrator's pre-merge legs are the scoped runs ruled on 2026-09-28: the targeted files the item touches, the one-environment `test-all` or `test-fast` leg the item's Accept line names, and the lint, format and typecheck tasks. They run on the branch, detached, through the gate lock (below). They are a confidence measure, not the gate: nothing in them is repeated on all environments at once.

**At the merge, CI.** After Peter's merge the orchestrator pushes `master` to `origin/v2` (and, once the `1.0.0b1` tag has moved the v2 line there, to `origin/master`). The CI run on that push (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`, with the path-gated jobs of the CI section above) is **the merged gate of record**. The status row records the run id and the per-job counts (`passed` / `skipped` per matrix cell, one line) where the five-suite summary lines used to go, and the handoff's "next gate's baselines" are that run's per-job counts, not a local machine's. A job that path filters skipped is recorded as skipped, not as a pass.

**A red run.** If the run on a merge is red, nothing else merges until it is green again, or until the failure is ruled flaky and the row says so. The worked example is the 2026-10-01 race: CI run 36935963691 on the W6.14 merge push failed one row on Python 3.12 only (`tests/core/test_simulate.py::TestThePoolIsReusedAcrossMapCalls::test_a_dead_worker_does_replace_the_pool`; a worker died between two `result()` calls and the next `submit` raised `BrokenProcessPool` outside the handler); it was diagnosed, fixed at `30d43e3` on its own branch, and the confirming run (36943679622 on `e09d530`) was green before the next merge. A rerun that turns green without a code change is recorded as "flaky, rerun green" with both run ids; one that does it twice for the same row is a defect to be filed, not a rerun to be repeated.

**The fallback for a machine without GitHub.** The scripts in `~/.cache/ampere-gates/` (`gate-leg.sh <env> [tag]` and the per-item `*-gate.sh` files beside it) remain the fallback when CI cannot be reached. Each is a detached shell chain that takes `/tmp/ampere-gate.lock` with `flock`, runs `pixi run -e <env> test-all` in the main checkout, and writes a summary log under the cache directory (not `/tmp`, so it survives a reboot). "The lock" is only that file: it is not a pytest fixture, `tests/conftest.py` has none, and it exists so that two five-suite runs, or a five-suite run and a docs build, never share a 13 GB machine. Launch one with `nohup setsid ~/.cache/ampere-gates/gate-leg.sh dev <tag> &` and read `<tag>.log` for the verdict; the row then quotes the local counts and says that they are the fallback.

**What CI never sees.** Two sets of rows are outside every CI run and are quoted in the row instead. The `hyperion` rows (the `hyperion` environment's radiative-transfer examples, run on the development machine with `pixi run -e hyperion ...`) are run by the orchestrator when a merge touches that code and the counts go in the row. The GPU rows (`tests/gpu/`, skipped without an accelerator) are W6.16's: the cluster procedure, run once on the beta tag, not per merge.

**Where this agrees with the rest.** `docs/orchestration.md`'s rules still hold: agents never run a five-suite gate, every long command is detached through the lock, and the orchestrator does the polling. What changes is only *where the merged gate runs*.

### The GPU rows on the cluster

*(W6.16. The first end-to-end run is **W6.5's release gate**, on the beta tag; building this procedure did not run it, and the item's acceptance does not include a run.)*

**What the rows are, and why CI never runs them.** `tests/gpu` is 33 API-level smoke rows after parametrisation (`test_torch_gpu.py`, `test_jax_gpu.py`) that skip wherever there is no accelerator, which is every ordinary machine and every GitHub runner: CI is CPU-only by ruling, so no CI job runs them and none will. They are the second of the two things the merged gate never sees (the first is the `hyperion` rows), so they run where the hardware is, once per release, and their summary line goes in the release's status row. They claim that `device=` reaches every tensor and array, that a problem composes, realises, differentiates and samples on an accelerator, and nothing about speed or CPU/GPU parity.

**The environment.** The pixi `gpu` environment is the one environment that carries both backends, deliberately: torch `2.14.1+cu126` from PyTorch's `cu126` index (the newest cu12x index publishing the torch version the other environments lock; `cu128` and `cu129` stop at 2.11 and 2.13) and `jax[cuda12]` 0.11.2, with `pyro-ppl` and `numpyro` beside them. Both are the **CUDA 12 wheel family**, which bundles its own CUDA libraries, so the node needs only a driver, not a toolkit module. It has its own solve group, so re-solving it cannot move any other environment's lock section, and it is **locked but never installed on the development machine** (a CUDA torch plus a CUDA jax is several gigabytes): `pixi lock` is the check there; `pixi install -e gpu --frozen` happens on the cluster only (frozen, not locked: `--locked` trips on pixi #7024, the editable project's `torch` extra against the lock's PyTorch index, as CI's `setup-pixi` steps already know — the first cluster bootstrap on 2026-10-05 hit it).

**The cluster.** The scripts under `scripts/cluster/` target a Slurm cluster that routes a job to the cluster of the login host it was submitted from and **names no partition, account or QoS in a job script** (site policy; the job asks only for `--gres=gpu:1`, 4 CPUs, 32 GB and 30 minutes). Files move through the data-mover hosts, never a login host; `/home` is a hard 20 GB quota, so pixi, its cache, the clone and the logs all live under the project space. Everything that identifies an account (the cluster's label, the ssh aliases, the user, the project ID, the paths) is read from `scripts/cluster/config.yaml`, which is gitignored; `config.example.yaml` is the committed template and every script refuses to run on its values. Expect to be asked for at least 75 % CPU utilisation; check a job with `sacct`, never a `watch squeue` loop.

**One-time bootstrap.** Copy `scripts/cluster/config.example.yaml` to `config.yaml` and fill it in, then run `scripts/cluster/run.sh bootstrap`. It lays out `<fred_dir>/ampere/`, installs a static pixi binary with `PIXI_HOME` and `PIXI_CACHE_DIR` there, generates an ed25519 deploy key on the login node and prints its public half. **Peter adds that key** at `https://github.com/ICSM/ampere/settings/keys` as a deploy key with *Allow write access* unchecked (the cluster only ever reads). Run `run.sh bootstrap` again: the clone now succeeds and `pixi install -e gpu --frozen` builds the environment. The code reaches the cluster by that clone, then `git fetch --tags && git checkout <tag>`, never by copying a tree.

**The per-release run** (the orchestrator, with Peter's word, against the tag the release gate names):

1. `scripts/cluster/run.sh submit <tag>`: it checks the tag out in the cluster's clone, shows the exact `gpu_rows.sbatch` that will run, asks for confirmation, submits, records the job ID in `~/.cache/ampere-gates/gpu/submissions.txt` and prints the `sacct` line to watch it with.
2. When the job is done, `scripts/cluster/run.sh fetch <tag>`: it copies the log back through the data-mover host to `~/.cache/ampere-gates/gpu/<tag>.log`.
3. Quote the pytest summary line `fetch` prints in the release's status row (the cluster appends a resource-usage epilogue after the job, so the summary is *not* the log's last line — `fetch` finds it by pattern); the log sits beside the status row as `~/.cache/ampere-gates/gpu/<tag>.log`.

**What the log must show.** In order: the header line (tag, cluster, job, node, UTC time); the `nvidia-smi` table (an A100 and its driver version); the `module spider cuda` listing (or the note that there is none); the driver line (`== driver <version>, floor 525`); the checkout line with the tag and the full hash; the pytest output (run with `pixi run --frozen`: a compute node has no network, and without the flag `pixi run` re-checks every environment first, trips on pixi #7024 and tries to re-solve one — job 18040399 on 2026-10-05 died exactly so before any row ran); the `== pytest exit status` line; the pytest summary; and then whatever the site appends (a resource-usage table on this cluster). A clean run has no failures, and the rows must be *passed*, not all skipped: a log whose summary reads "33 skipped" means the wheels found no accelerator and the run proves nothing. **The first run that reached the rows (job 18041011, 2026-10-05) read "13 failed, 20 passed"** — three faults no CPU can show (the realisation self-check's numpy conversion of a CUDA tensor, `jax.devices()` listing only the default backend, a test helper's hard-coded width), fixed by W6.18; that is what the rows are for.

**The driver floor.** The CUDA 12 wheels need a driver of at least **525** (NVIDIA's minor-version compatibility for CUDA 12). The cluster's driver and toolkit versions were not known when the environment was locked; the job's first act is therefore `nvidia-smi` and `module spider cuda`, written to the log, and it **refuses to continue (exit status 3) if the driver is below the floor**. An A100 node on AlmaLinux 9 carries a 12-capable driver in every plausible configuration. If a node proves otherwise, the log still holds what `nvidia-smi` reported: re-solve the `gpu` feature against that (a cu11x torch index and `jax[cuda11]` for an old driver, or the cluster's own CUDA module through `modules:` in `config.yaml` for a mismatch the bundled libraries cannot cover), re-lock, and say so in the release's status row.

### Parallel test runs (`pytest-xdist`): the audit

Measured 2026-10-02 on the 16-core, 13 GB development machine (shared with a docs build and another agent, so load averages of 4 to 13 and every figure is good to perhaps 10 to 15 %), `dev` environment, each suite alone through the gate lock, `pytest tests/<suite> -q -p no:cacheprovider [-n 4]`. The figures are pytest's own wall time (pixi's start-up and any wait for the lock excluded). **`-n 4` is the most this machine can say anything about; the speed-up at `-n auto` on CI's runners is a separate measurement**, and `-n auto` is not used: runner and development machine differ, and `auto` on 16 cores with 13 GB is a memory hazard for the torch and jax legs.

| Suite | Serial | `-n 4`, default BLAS threads | Counts (passed / skipped), serial and `-n 4` | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `core` | 57 s | 31 s | 1417 / 30, equal | parallel |
| `results` | 684 s | 534 s | 481 / 5, equal | parallel (a few long rows bound it) |
| `conformance` | 29 s | 14 s | 677 / 79, equal | parallel |
| `backends` | 20 s | 16 s | 149 / 65, equal | parallel |
| `inference` | 169 s | 169 s | 214 / 380, equal | parallel, no gain (one long row bounds it) |
| `examples` | 286 s | 217 s | 203 / 30, equal | parallel (the `ProcessExecutor` rows of `test_cstar` and `test_external_simulator` pass inside workers; their processes were not counted) |
| `m2` | 558 s | 304 s | 115 / 32, equal | parallel (the agreement margins held) |
| `interferometry` | 127 s, 145 s | 300 s, 339 s | 21 / 9, equal | **slower: left serial** (pinned to one worker) |
| `astrometry` | 406 s | 452 s | 14 / 14, equal | **slower: left serial** (pinned to one worker) |
| torch `conformance` | 65 s | 43 s | 1087 / 79, equal | parallel |
| jax `conformance` | 339 s | 177 s | 1087 / 79, equal | parallel (each worker re-pays the jit compilation) |

**Correctness.** Every suite passes with counts identical to the serial run, in all three environments for `conformance` (the cross-check that the backends' seeded streams survive: the streams are per problem, `FittingProblem(seed=)`, never a process-global generator). No row failed or flaked under `-n 4`. A read of every test and example for writes outside `tmp_path` (`grep -rn "\.cache\|expanduser\|/tmp/" tests/ --include=*.py`, and the cache users `SBIEngine(cache=)` and `tests/examples/test_cached_fit.py`) found none: the cached-fit example is given `cache_dir=tmp_path`, `functools.cache` in the kernels module is per process and harmless, and `git status` after the runs is clean. The "shared lock" the item names is the gate lock above, which exists outside pytest.

**Speed.** Two suites are correct but slower in parallel, and `-n 4` with the default thread counts also oversubscribed the cores (each numpy process opens its own BLAS thread pool). `interferometry` rebuilds three class-scoped calibration fits (about 100 s of setup each) in every worker that draws one of their rows; with one BLAS thread per process it ran in 103 s at `-n 4`, but it still gains nothing over a single worker. `astrometry`'s wall clock is bounded by two nested-sampling rows (about 270 s and 160 s) whatever the worker count (444 s at `-n 4` with one BLAS thread). Both are therefore **left serial inside the parallel run**: `tests/conftest.py` marks every row of each with `xdist_group` and the tasks pass `--dist loadgroup`, so each suite runs in one worker while the others proceed on the rest. The tasks also set `OMP_NUM_THREADS`, `OPENBLAS_NUM_THREADS` and `MKL_NUM_THREADS` to 1: `test-fast` took 294 s serial, 260 s at `-n 4` with default threads and 152 s with them pinned.

**Adopted.** `test-all` and `test-fast` run `-n 4 --dist loadgroup` with single-thread BLAS. `test-all` (dev): 36 min 19 s serial (W6.1's merged gate, 3290 passed / 645 skipped) against **19 min 40 s** (3290 / 645, identical); `test-fast` (dev): 4 min 54 s serial (`-n 0`, today's tree) against **2 min 32 s** (2951 / 444). `-n 0` on the command line restores a serial run; CI's jobs do not use these two tasks (they use the `test-group-*` tasks and explicit `pytest` lines), so CI is unchanged. **Proposal for W6.3 or the orchestrator, not made here**: add `-n auto --dist loadgroup` to CI's suites steps only after a measurement on a runner, since the `ci.yml` jobs are not this item's to edit.

## The release procedure

*(W6.5. Written for `1.0.0b1`; every later release follows the same steps
with its own tag. **The tag, the PyPI approval, the GitHub release, the push
of `master` to `origin/master`, branch protection and the Read the Docs
settings are Peter's, and agents never do them** — ground rule 3. The
orchestrator's steps are (a) and (h), plus the TestPyPI dry run before them.)*

**What is already in place.** The distribution is `ampere-astro` (D13), the
import name `ampere`. The version comes from git: setuptools_scm counts only
`v*` tags (`pyproject.toml`'s `[tool.setuptools_scm.scm.git]`), so a build
before the beta is a development version `0.2.devN` (the legacy `v0.1` tag is
the last `v*` tag below it) and the `v1.0.0b1` tag builds as exactly
`1.0.0b1` — **provided the tree is clean when setuptools_scm runs**: a dirty
tree at a tag versions as the *next* pre-release's `.dev0`. The workflow's
build runs under `pixi run --frozen` for exactly that reason: a plain
`pixi run` on the runner's pixi (0.81 on 2026-10-05; 0.68 at home never
did) rewrites `pixi.lock` — it adds a `virtual-packages` block to the
platform entry — before the task starts, and the first run on the `v1.0.0b1`
tag (37259563688) built `1.0.0b2.dev0` and was stopped by the
tag-builds-its-own-version check; a clean-tree check after the build now
names the modified file. `pixi run build-dist && pixi run check-dist`
builds and checks the sdist and wheel locally. `.github/workflows/release.yml` publishes through
**trusted publishing** (OIDC): the pending publishers for `ampere-astro` on
TestPyPI and PyPI name owner `ICSM`, repository `ampere`, workflow
`release.yml` and environments `testpypi` and `pypi` — those four strings are
load-bearing, and no token or secret exists or may be added. Zenodo's switch
for the repository is on, so the first GitHub *release* (not a bare tag)
mints the DOI. If either GitHub environment restricts which refs may deploy
to it, `testpypi` must admit the `v*` tags and the branch a dry run is
dispatched from (`v2`, later `master`), and `pypi` the `v*` tags. The changelog (`docs/source/changelog.rst`) and the citation
(`ampere/_citation.py`, `CITATION.cff`, `docs/source/citing.rst`) are written
for `1.0.0b1`.

**The dry run, after W6.5 merges** (the orchestrator, once, on Peter's word;
it needs `release.yml` on the default branch, which is why the `master`
switch of (f) came first — D15): push `master` to `origin/master`, then
`gh workflow run release.yml --ref master -f target=testpypi`. It builds the
merged commit as `0.2.devN` (the upload drops setuptools_scm's `+g<sha>`
local label, which PyPI and TestPyPI refuse), publishes it to TestPyPI and
installs it there in ten fresh environments — the base install, each extra of
`all` on its own, and `all` itself. TestPyPI is a scratch index, so the
development version is harmless; the run never reaches the `pypi` job. A red
run is fixed by an ordinary PR before (a).

**The release, in order:**

- **(a) The release gate — the orchestrator.** On the candidate commit (the
  head of `master`, pushed to `origin/v2`): all four legs, through CI's run on
  that push (the gate of record, "The merged gate" above) or the lock's
  fallback scripts, plus the GPU rows on the cluster ("The GPU rows on the
  cluster" above), with the pytest summary `fetch` prints quoted in the release row.
  `scripts/cluster/run.sh submit` checks out whatever ref it is given, so
  before the tag exists it takes the gated commit's hash (and reads
  `gpu_rows.sbatch` from that checkout, so a fix to the job script moves the
  candidate) (on `origin/v2`, and
  so fetched by the cluster's clone); if Peter would rather the rows ran on
  the tag itself, they run between (b) and (d) instead, and the PyPI approval
  waits for their log.
  If the cluster cannot run them, the release row says the GPU rows were not
  run and why; the release does not wait on them unless Peter says so. Then
  the orchestrator tells Peter the gate is green and hands him the commands
  for (b).
- **(b) Peter tags and pushes the tag.** On `master` at the gated commit:
  `git tag -a v1.0.0b1 -m "ampere 1.0.0b1"` and `git push origin v1.0.0b1`.
  The tag is pushed alone — not `--tags`, which would also push the local
  `spec-v1.0` and nothing else should go with it.
- **(c) CI and the release workflow run on the tag.** `ci.yml` runs every job
  on the tag (a tag push is never path-gated), including the `-W` docs build
  Read the Docs repeats. `release.yml` builds, checks that the tag built
  exactly `1.0.0b1`, publishes to TestPyPI, smoke-installs every extra from
  there, and then **waits at the `pypi` environment** for approval.
- **(d) Peter approves; PyPI publishes.** In the run's page on GitHub
  (*Review deployments* → `pypi` → *Approve*). Check the smoke jobs are green
  first. Once published, a version cannot be re-uploaded to PyPI, even after
  deletion: a broken release is fixed by `1.0.0b2`, never by replacing
  `1.0.0b1`.
- **(e) Peter creates the GitHub release from the tag**
  (`gh release create v1.0.0b1 --prerelease --title "ampere 1.0.0b1" --notes
  "…"` with the notes pointing at the changelog page, or the web UI). Zenodo
  archives it and mints the version DOI and the concept DOI.
- **(f) Branch protection on `master`** (D4). *The move itself happened
  before the dry run, not here* — **D15 (ruled by Peter 2026-10-05)**: GitHub
  registers a `workflow_dispatch` workflow only from the default branch, so
  `release.yml` could not be dispatched while `origin/master` was the legacy
  line; the orchestrator fast-forwarded `origin/master` from the legacy head
  `b8e585b` to the v2 line at `fef26ca` on Peter's word on 2026-10-05, a
  plain fast-forward with no force, and from then on `origin/master` is what
  the orchestrator pushes at each merge; the `v2` mirror's purpose ended there
  (its `ci.yml` trigger is left for a later housekeeping). Then branch protection
  on `master` (repository settings → Branches, or a ruleset), as D4 rules
  it — **CI required, no force-push** — and no more: require the CI checks
  by name — `lint + format-check`, `actionlint`,
  `typecheck (pyrefly, new namespaces)`, the `test (py…)` matrix, the
  `new-namespace suites (…)` matrices, the `typecheck (pyrefly, …)` backend
  legs, `docs build` and `minimal install (no extras)` (the path-gated ones
  report success when skipped, so requiring them is safe) — block
  force-pushes and block deletion. **Do not require a pull request**: merges
  are made locally and pushed (the working agreement), so that setting would
  block every push. Leave the administrator bypass as GitHub sets it
  (the classic rule's "Do not allow bypassing" unchecked): the pushes come
  from Peter's account, and the required checks are then the gate of record
  on each push, as "The merged gate" above already has them, rather than a
  hard block on the push itself.
- **(g) Peter points Read the Docs at the new line.** In the project's admin:
  default branch `master` (so `latest` follows it), and the `v1.0.0b1`
  version activated (the integration builds every tag, but a version must be
  active to be served). `stable` stays empty until a final release: Read the
  Docs never promotes a pre-release to it.
- **(h) The record — the orchestrator.** Dates the decision-log row "The
  beta release `1.0.0b1` (W6.5)" (`DATE`, `TAG_COMMIT`) and fills W6.5's
  status row's gate fields (the CI run on the tag, the release run, the GPU
  log). Then the first post-beta change lands as one commit: the concept DOI
  into `ampere/_citation.py` (`"doi"`, and `"date_released"`), `CITATION.cff`
  regenerated with `scripts/write_citation_cff.py`, and a DOI badge on the
  README. The orchestrator also closes issues #57–#60 and #62 with their
  one-line pointers (D10).

**Every later release** repeats (a)–(e) and (h): a changelog section for the
new version written before the gate, then the tag. (f) and (g) happen once.
